MUSICAL AND DRAMATIC RECITAL AT WARRINGTON.

A joint recital in which an able elocutionist and an equally gifted pianist were associated wits given in the Co-operative Hall, Sankey-street,'on Saturday evening before a delighted and numerous audience. The entertainment was promoted by our townsman Mr.T.C.London, now the energetic and popular pas- tor of the new Windsor Congregational Church Sal- ford. The two ladies Miss Florence. Bourne and Mdlle. Szumowska upon whom the whole burden of the evening devo!ved are not unknown to fame in their respective capacities of elocutionist and pianist. But notwithstanding this fact it was pretty clear that the Warrington audience was to a certain extent taken by surprise by the excellence of the perferma,nces of both these accomplished artistes, and after the good impres- sion they have left behind it may he safely predicted that en a return visit they would be enthusiastically welcomed. Mdlle Szurnowska is a pianist whose touch is remarkable alike for its energy and its delicacy. Hot command of technique is indeed so complete as toenil- her to rank as a most apt pupil of her distinguished master the renowned Paderewski. Faultless expression and a high degree of intelligence characterised her in- , terpretaeion of the varied items comprised in the pro- gramme and the audience was loud in the demonstra- tions of its appreciation and approval. Beethoven's Smata in E flat major ; four excerpts from the works o! Chopin ; Nocturne Paderewski, Fileuso, Wagner-Lizt, and Valse Strause-Tausig, form an array of plece<i suffi- ciently deverse in style to try the powers of any virtuoso, and it is no exaggerated compliment to say that Mdlle. e4zumowska proved herself equal to the exigencies of a due interpretation of each and every item. Miss Florence Bourne is a clever actress,as well as an elocutionist. The - intense feeling and dramatic power with which she in- vested some of her recitals reached a climax in Tenny- son's " Rizpah." On the other hand her daintiness and quaintness of style was most appropriately apparent in "The Bob o' Link," " What my Lover said," and " Mony Musk." The audience was highly delighted with this portion of the entertainment, and were often enthusiastic in its tokens of satisfaction. The " Salford Chronicle" in an article on the new Windsor Congregational Church thus refers to Mr. Len- don :—" Up to recent years the Church had not attained any nsrticular degree of prominence, and it was not un- til the ministry of Mr. T. C. London, its present pastel', that any serious elfcrt seems to ha ve been put forth to lift it from the well-worn conventional rut. Educated at Liverpool College under Deau Howson, vibe is known as the joint author, with Professor Cony- hearn, of a standard work on "Time Ufe and Epistles of St. Paul." and afterwards under Dr. George Butler, who was made Canon of Winchester, and whose wife, Mrs. Josephine Butler, is distinguished for her social work, Theodore Craig London was called to the church so re- cently as March 1353, and at once assumed the pastor- ate of the Now Windsor flock, in succession to the Rev. William Briddon, who had officiated during the previous four years. He has now, therefore, ent, rod upon his fifth year, and it may safely be hazarded that no minis- ter in Salford is more beloved by his congregation than Mr. London, but his high literary culture and the grati- fying success which has attended his efforts to popularise the Church find recognition among a much wider circle than that in the immediate vicinity of New Windsor. In the pulpit there is nothing of the factious demagogue about his style of oratory, his subjects are treated in a thoroughly up-to-date fashion,and his points are forcibly argued out to their logical conclusion in fi q oiet,yet none the less conviecing manner. Toe New Windsor educa- tional lectures are now firmly a established institution in Salford."

A Shevrsbury correspondent telegraphs :—On luesday afternoon a box was received by the station-master at Preesgweene, between Shrewsbury and Chester, which was found to contain a baby, with a request that he would adopt it. A. LIBERAL CANDIDATE FOR ST.

HELENS. At a full meeting of the executive committee of the St. Helens Liberal Association on Thursday night a letter was read from Councillor John Firster, chairman of the executive committee, who had been invited to become the Liberal candidate for the borough at the next elec- tion, stating that if the Liberal Association as a body were unanimous in' selecting him, he was prepared to place his services at the disposal of the party. The letter was received with great enthusiasm by all present, and it was decided to call a sp.ci tl meeting of all Liberalit next Thursday, and place Mr. ForstAr'S name before There is co doubt Mr. F.,rster will he unanimously adopted, and that a ken fight will take place.

IMPORTANT POSTAL CHANGES AT PARLESTOWN- As the outcome of some correspondence which Dr. Mouncey had with the Postal Authorities HI London, as far back as the Autumn of last year, with regard to the postal arrangements in Earles- town, more particularly having reference to the times for the collection and delivery of letters, all Inspector waited upon him on Thursday, and ex- plained to Dr. Mouncey the changes which the Department proposed to make,and which will come into operation on the first Monday in Niel-. The changes will be seen at a glance to be most impor- tant, to say nothing of the immense convenience§ which will result, and Dr. Menticey deserves the heartiest thanks (ef his fellow townsmen for his efforts. Certain alterations in the structure of the Earlestown Post Office are to be made, and the collection ot letters will be greatly altered. The last post at night is 8 o'clock, this has bees put an hour later, and letters can be posted up to 9 o'clock in the evening. The next co!lection and despatch will be at 5 o'clock in the morning, again at 8.45, 9.55, 11.55 for local deliveries, the letters posted at 11.55 for the town will be delivered at one o'clock. The despatch of letters on Sunday will be put back an hour, and they can be posted 'up to 5.30 p.m., instead of 430. All letters 10 future will be sorted and despatched trom Earles- town in future instead of Newton.—These are most important changes, and the inhabitants will doubt- less profit thereby.

IS MARRIAGE A FAILURE? What a question to ask, but how often could it be answered in the affirmative, the nrin, ipal cause being the improvidence lof Furnishing on the Hire System. Since cur advertisements have appeared pointing out the disadvantages we have re- ceived several le ten agreeing with our denunciations of this pernicious sys em. Earls Hire Agreement specifies that after the deposit is paid, so maey wt,ekly payments shall be made and that until the whole of the sum mentioned is paid the , goods shall not become the property of the would-be pur- chaser. and that if any of the payments are negb-cted to the exact time specified the goods may be forcibly taken away and the whole of the paym:nts forfeited. County Court judges have denounced the sy,tem, inasmuch as the vendor I gets both money and goods. Again, the pri,es charged are enormous, without any respect being paid to the quality, in many cases being worn out before they are paid for. To corn- , hat this system, and open the eyes of the public, Worsley's of "The Pantheon," Gayt horn, Manchester, the Largest Retailers of Second-hand Furniture in the worlal, have determined to ailow all respectable householders to have furniture at the ticketed marked cash price, a small percentage being charged for the convemence of payment by the week, month, or quatter. Furniture from £5 to £1 00) may he arranged for in this. way.
